# Product checklist

**The single current work list. Restructured 19 October 2026.** Builders and agents update this file in the same increment as the work: tick an item only when its stated scope is implemented and checked on actual sessions; add new feedback here before starting it. STATUS is the session log, CONTINUITY is the handoff, numbered reviews are evidence. They are not competing task lists.

**Claiming.** Before starting an item, claim its ID in the [STATUS](STATUS.md#claimed) table and push that to `main`; `node scripts/claims.ts` shows what is open and what is taken.

**How to read it.** §1 is the **priority order**: one line per item with its ID and tags. §3 holds the **detail** for every ID, grouped by category: problem, what to do, acceptance, where in the code. §4 archives every item completed before this restructure, verbatim.

`[x]` = implemented and locally checked within the stated limits. `[ ]` = unfinished. A checked item does not certify native accounts, every device, visual preference or release readiness.

Evidence for this restructure: [122 developer review](review/122-developer-product-review.md) (with builder corrections), [123 harness data audit](review/123-harness-data-audit.md), [124 Sacred Timeline spec](review/124-sacred-timeline-git-graph.md).

**19 October investigation candidate:** [107](review/107-investigation-finishing.md) records the bounded workflow/recovery/control increment reconciled with main54f93af. New P0-P3 direction is retained. Signed-in native checks remain builder-owned; broad UI/motion/platform items are not certified by a synthetic matrix.

---

## 0. Rules for every item

1. **Product north star.** Drishya is the best *visualization of agent work* plus the *developer tools needed to understand it while it happens*. A developer should understand the change as it is made, not only after. Every item must serve that.
2. **Minimal by default.** The builder's design rule: the UI stays minimalistic and calm.
   - Do **not** add resident buttons or panels for a new feature. Put it in an existing owner (contextual hover, the ⋯ menu, Ctrl+K, a reader toolbar) unless it *replaces* something.
   - Any surface whose controls take more space than its content is a bug (see UI-5).
3. **Verify before changing.** Use the running app on actual Codex and Claude sessions and read the code before "fixing" anything. Several things that look wrong are deliberate (builder corrections, 19 Oct):
   - Toolbox→card connectors appear **on hover only**.
   - The passage layout **wraps** actions onto the next row with arrows from box to box. This is intentional; one infinite horizontal lane is rejected.
   - **File peek** (beside the agent) and **Workspace** (full view when not watching the agent) are two intended modes.
   - The **3D repository map** stays as an aesthetic surface for now.
4. **Truth.** Captured ≠ derived. A model's output is always labelled as generated and cites captured evidence. Missing data stays visibly missing. No invented links or locations ([AGENTS](../AGENTS.md) constraints).
5. **Every interaction has purposeful motion.** Every click, toggle, open and close gets a suitable, interruptible transition tied to its true origin, with reduced-motion and low-resource fallbacks (MOTION-1). Use the installed `emil-design-eng`, `animate`, `review-animations` and `animation-performance` skills.
6. **One coherent, verified increment per commit,** so either builder can backtrack. Schema changes need both builders' agreement (DATA-10).
7. **Tick, then sync.** Tick the item's own checkbox in §3, then run `node scripts/sync-tasks.ts`: it sets the ☑/☐ marks and counts in the priority tables below (`--check` verifies them).
8. **The premium bar (Yash, 29 Oct).** Every surface you touch is clean and premium down to the smallest detail, and information reaches the user with ease (no item-by-item paging, text that says what it is). Product target: $10,000 end to end, for millions of people who only read agent text today. Full statement: [VISION §8](VISION.md), *The premium bar*. Order after SCREEN-1: AUDIT-1, the UI passes, then the motion passes, COMP-1, VIZ-1.
9. **Choosing a model.**
   - Opus for design specs, ambiguous UI direction and reviews.
   - Sonnet is suitable for executing items here that have explicit **Accept** lines.
   - Either model must verify in the browser on actual sessions before ticking.

### Dependency gates and retest triggers

Keep dependent work here **and** under its own item; this is a cross-reference within the single checklist, not a second queue. “Free” in `claims.ts` means unclaimed, not necessarily ready. When a dependency merges/becomes available, the next agent checks this table, claims the affected item, reruns its stated acceptance on fresh/redacted real data and records the result. A merged dependency alone does not tick the dependent item. Keep the row until the retest passes; add newly discovered gates as work proceeds.

| Dependent item / remaining scope | Required evidence or dependency | Retest when available |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PERF-2: large-journal resource/latency gate | PERF-1 measurements; coordinate claimed DATA-1/2 ingest/store/projection changes first | Re-run complete-import and append/browser matrices after capture merges; preserve exact IDs/order and all content. Restore RSS and measured interaction budgets before new feature work; retain physical/platform checks separately. |
| READ-1 and TURN-1: Codex edit line numbers and tests hidden inside wrappers | DATA-1 typed FileChange / CommandExecution items | Re-import dense Codex session; inspect true hunk line numbers and runner outcomes, ensure wrapper/child deduplication and turn counts match captured evidence. Existing checked scope remains bounded. |
| CONTENT-9: explicit missing-reasoning state | Reasoning-state slice S3 in review125; shared contract work DATA-1/DATA-2 must be coordinated first | One quiet missing-text line for encrypted/signature-only blocks; genuine summaries/text remain visible, no line when no reasoning block was recorded; live, replay and both harnesses. |
| CONTENT-3 / CONTENT-1 / CONTENT-2: historical questions, images and web evidence | DATA-1 typed items plus review125 S4 / DATA-4; inline-image byte policy D7 needs both builders' decision | Verify pending → answered/cancelled historical states and supported native replies; mixed image/text and browser results retain exact raw access. Use a fresh sample, not tool definitions. |
| CONTENT-4: additional plan/task sources | DATA-7 and a real Claude TaskCreate/TaskUpdate/ExitPlanMode sample (review125 D10) | Ten or more captured updates show one evolving per-turn checklist, revisions remain reachable, no invented task statuses. Existing TodoWrite can be tested separately. |
| CONTENT-5: native sub-agent identities | DATA-1 / DATA-3 retained names, model, effort and lineage | Actual Codex and Claude parent/child sessions; distinguish source-linked from enclosing-call correlation, retain unresolved lineage honestly. |
| HOME-1 stats / LIVE-4 context and limits | DATA-2 usage plus DATA-3 metadata where required | Fresh real sessions and enrichment; reported vs derived labels, missing data hidden, context denominators/limit resets correct, no current-page totals mistaken for full history. |
| NATIVE-1..4 and REL-2/REL-3 broad acceptance | Real signed-in native harnesses, multi-agent/conflict samples, physical lower-resource/phone/accessibility environment | Repeat the corresponding end-to-end and freeze/truth/ten-minute checks. Synthetic transports and viewport emulation do not close these gates. |


**Tags:**
- `bug` defect
- `live` live loop / "now"
- `turn` turns and navigation
- `read` reading diffs, code and output
- `content` content-shaped presentation per action type
- `data` harness capture/adapters
- `explain` model-derived explanation
- `git` Git review, graph and timeline
- `files` Explorer, peek and Workspace
- `ui` visual system, chrome, navigation and minimalism
- `motion` interaction motion
- `home` Home and session discovery
- `review` Review work, search and packets
- `map` repository map
- `native` native controller acceptance
- `perf` performance and scale
- `rel` release and platform

**Priority:**
- **P0:** now.
- **P1:** next.
- **P2:** later.
- **P3:** deferred ideas.

**Build order (20 Oct).** Inside each table, work top to bottom and skip an item only when it is claimed or needs something that is not done. The order is by dependency, so nothing is built twice:

1. **Capture first.** DATA-1…DATA-9 change what the harnesses record; every view below stands on them.
2. **Views that consume that data.** CONTENT-*, FILES-1, REVIEW-1, HOME-1, READ-6/8, GIT-2/4, LIVE-4.
3. **Structure** (UI-1, READ-4, FILES-3…5, REVIEW-4) and native acceptance (NATIVE-*).
4. **Final passes, once, at the end:** UI-4, UI-2, UI-7, MOTION-1, MOTION-3, UI-3, UI-8, then REL-*. Do not start these while feature items above are open; polishing a surface that is still changing is done twice. Small defects found earlier are fixed in place, but the passes themselves wait.
5. **Then the P3 ideas,** including IDEA-6 (AI-written explanations, parked).

**Order after SCREEN-1 (Yash, 29 Oct).** Once the feature items are done, the order is fixed:

1. **SCREEN-1** (computer use on a big screen), with NATIVE work on whatever sessions Yash can supply. **Amendment (Yash, 30 Oct):** AUDIT-1 starts only when the remaining screen, content, files and UI items above it have landed: SCREEN-1, CONTENT-12, FILES-7, UI-1b and the claimed FILES-8, UI-16, CONTENT-8. FILES-5's remaining external platform acceptance moves with MAC-1 and does not hold the audit. **All of those landed by 31 Oct** (SCREEN-1, CONTENT-12, FILES-7, FILES-8, UI-1b, UI-16, CONTENT-8, plus CONTENT-13/14 and TEST-2's two causes); AUDIT-1 starts when Yash says. FILES-9 (an edited or written file must always open to a full view) was added on 31 Oct and is the first Pass A item.
2. **AUDIT-1:** one pass through every function that exists, as a developer would use it on real sessions, listing what is wrong in the UI, the backend handling, the displayed content and the way a user gets to information. It runs *before* any UI pass, because the UI passes would otherwise polish surfaces whose content is still unclear. It is not fitted to Yash's own list; his findings are the minimum, and what he does not name is audited too.
3. **One UI pass first** (UI-8, which absorbs UI-7, UI-3 and UI-4b: Yash, 30 Oct; [brief](review/ui-pass-brief.md)), fed by AUDIT-1's findings: layout, space, type, content presentation and every small detail settle before anything is animated.
4. **Then the motion passes** (MOTION-1, MOTION-3), on the finished UI (Yash, 29 Oct): motion is tuned to surfaces that will not move again; animating a layout that is still changing is done twice.
5. **The fresh-eyes agent review (COMP-1):** a new agent that sees the whole product as a would-be user.
6. **VIZ-1:** task-shaped visualizations and the dynamics that make it a premium end-to-end product.

First the base; the ideas in VIZ-1 are recorded now and decided after the review.

**The whole-product passes, planned (Yash, 31 Oct: "plan it your own way"; features, then UI, then motion, kept separate).** All pre-audit items have landed (SCREEN-1, CONTENT-12/13/14, FILES-7/8, UI-1b, UI-16, CONTENT-8, TEST-2's two causes). The passes run one after the other, each ending in a gate on real sessions before the next begins, because each pass hands the next one a settled surface:

1. **Pass A, features (AUDIT-1, then what it finds).** The user's-eye audit of what exists and what is missing, then the building of it, so the backend and the data are ready and complete before anything is styled. Three parts, in order:
   - **A1, the audit as a user.** For each surface, on real sessions of both harnesses at 600 and 1440 px, live and replay: what would a person using this product need here, does the feature give it, does it help or only exist, and what would help that is not there. The journeys are the unit, not the components: open a session, read what the agent did, follow one file, one command, one failure to the end, review a change, search, return later, watch a live session. Findings are whole TASKS items (features to add, features to cut or merge, backend gaps).
   - **A2, the interaction inventory.** Every component and every kind of interaction it accepts (click, double click, right click, hover, focus and Tab, keyboard keys, scroll and wheel, drag, Escape, selection and copy, resize, the same during live and replay, while loading, empty and failing), written down with what the product does for each today, on real sessions. A missing or dead interaction, an inconsistent one and one that surprises are findings. This file is the checklist the UI and motion passes must also satisfy, so nothing is redesigned without knowing what it handles.
   - **A3, build the findings** in dependency order (data and backend first, then the views that read them), each a whole item with its own acceptance, then one integrated gate.
2. **Pass B, UI (UI-8).** On the finished features: concept first per surface, then every component, page, section and state, with the interaction inventory as the checklist (hover, focus, active, disabled, empty, loading, error for each), against the premium bar, using the skills in the brief.
3. **Pass C, motion (MOTION-1, MOTION-3).** On the finished UI, from the same inventory: every interaction's transition, nothing animated that carries no information, then the ten-minute calm check on live and replay.
4. **Then COMP-1** (a fresh-eyes review of the whole), and VIZ-1.

**Not yet to the mark (Yash, 5 Nov):** after ten minutes in the app he found the composer, its menus, the session menu and Usage too basic or full of facts nobody reads, motion that opens but does not close (or the reverse), and layout that does not use large screens. The audit, UI and motion passes are not finished until every feature, component and pixel meets the premium bar; UI-27 to UI-29, CONTENT-16, CONTENT-17, CONNECT-3, LAYOUT-1 and MOTION-4 hold what he found, and AUDIT-1 looks for the rest.

**The finishing pass (Yash, 6 Nov).** The remaining audit, UI and motion work is done **one component at a time with all three lenses at once**, so each component is finished once, to the fullest: features, presentation and motion decided together, then one integrated pass over the whole. FINISH-0 (shared foundations) comes first; FINISH-1 to FINISH-12 follow in order; FINISH-13 is the integrated pass and ends with MOTION-3's evaluation and AUDIT-1's close. Each FINISH item absorbs the open items for its surface and closes them when it merges. Opus walks, decides, writes the spec and verifies; a fresh Sonnet session builds each spec. The lenses are Impeccable, `emil-design-eng` and Taste's rules, plus `break-ui` and the motion skills. Method, order, lenses and the common acceptance: [finish-pass.md](review/finish-pass.md). UI-26 is paused until FINISH-12 merges.

**Clarity (Yash, 6 Nov, later).** After seeing the Home concepts ("clean and modern and takes accurate space, minimal but professional and premium"), Yash asked for that look on every page, component, menu and dropdown, built as a **new UI version, Clarity**, with Modern kept unchanged as the backup in Settings, and full freedom over the agent visualization, themes and palettes. Every FINISH item lands in Clarity; Modern changes only through shared content logic. Clarity becomes the default at FINISH-13. Home is concept A, Ledger + Now. Architecture and the guard: [clarity-1-version.md](review/finish/clarity-1-version.md); the look: VISION §9.

**Who decides (Yash, 4 Nov):** the audit's findings and the UI and motion decisions in all three passes are Claude's to make, and AUDIT-1 is Claude's to finish: the feature audit (A1), the written interaction inventory (A2) and the building of its findings (A3). Passes B and C landed before A was finished, so AUDIT-1's remaining walk (live, the review tools, the peek, compare) and the inventory also check the finished UI and motion, and anything they find becomes a whole item.

Each pass starts by reading the ledger in the [UI pass brief](review/ui-pass-brief.md) and appending to it; findings that belong to a later pass are recorded there, not lost. A feature found missing during Pass B or C goes back to Pass A's list as an item rather than being built inside a polish pass.

**How the UI and motion passes are run (Yash, 30 Oct).** One surface at a time, in this order, so that each tool does what it is good at and nothing is polished twice:

1. **AUDIT-1's findings for that surface** are the work list; the passes do not invent their own.
2. **Reviewer lens: the HIG skill** ([dickwu/apple-design-skill](https://github.com/dickwu/apple-design-skill), 123 Apple Human Interface Guideline pages plus a design-craft lens; no licence file in the repository and the pages are Apple's text, so it is installed for the user or cloned under `.local/`, never copied into this repository). Load only the always-load set (accessibility, layout, typography, color, designing for macOS) plus the 3 to 6 pages for what is on screen (sidebars, split views, toolbars, lists and tables, search fields, windows, motion, feedback and loading states), and cite file and heading. Desktop pages only; phone, touch, widgets, Apple Pay and Duo pages are out of scope here. **Its findings are advice, filtered through our doctrine:** Apple's Liquid Glass and translucency do not override the flat, hairline theme with blur only at the header/composer edges (AGENTS creative doctrine 6), and Apple's own warning applies: delight is not decoration.
3. **Worst-case data: `break-ui`** on the surface: long names, one-letter names, thousands of items, zero items, missing fields, non-Latin text. This is the tool for the smallest things in the premium bar.
4. **Craft: `emil-design-eng`** for composition and polish, and the installed **`apple-design`** skill for how things should feel to touch (springs, interruptibility, momentum, reduced motion).
5. **Component libraries (UI-3, Uiverse and others):** only to replace a specific component the audit found weak, never to add one; copy the CSS or markup, add no dependency, check each component's licence, and run it through the freeze, truth and ten-minute tests like anything else.
6. **Then the motion pass, after the UI pass is done:** `find-animation-opportunities` to list what should move and what should not, `improve-animations` for the plan, `animate` and `review-animations` to build and check, `animation-performance` for frame budget. The AGENTS rules hold: motion carries information about origin, relationship or change, or it is cut.
7. **Verify** at 600 and 1440 px on real sessions of both harnesses.

**One pass, concept first (Yash, 30 Oct).** UI-3, UI-4b, UI-7 and UI-8 are one pass (UI-8) over **every component, page and section that comes up**, done as a professional front-end and UX designer would, not component by component in isolation. For each surface the audit answers, in writing, what it is for, what the developer must understand there, what identifies each item, what goes where and how much room it gets, which edges it needs, which controls are primary, how a window opens and what the user returns to, and what its words say (the ten questions are in the [brief](review/ui-pass-brief.md), section 3). Foundations (the token sheet, the spacing, type, radius and border scales, the anatomy of a row, a toolbar, a panel) come first so every surface is built on them. The builder is not a UI expert and expects the advanced thinking from the agent; AUDIT-1, UI-8 and the motion passes may run on Opus.

**Cross-phase ledger (Yash, 30 Oct).** What one phase finds that belongs to another (an audit finding that is a motion idea, a UI decision that changes the backend) is appended to the ledger in the [brief](review/ui-pass-brief.md), section 5, and becomes a TASKS item when it needs work. Every phase reads the ledger first and appends to it last, and thinks through the whole of AUDIT, UI and motion rather than only its own part.

**Two more skill sets for the audit, UI and motion passes (Yash, 30 Oct; read from their READMEs only, nothing installed or run yet).**

- **[Impeccable](https://github.com/pbakaus/impeccable)** (Apache-2.0, about 96,000 stars, pushed 29 Oct): a design vocabulary of 24 commands plus a deterministic detector of 62 "slop" rules (no model, no key). **Use for the audit and the UI pass, as a reviewer:** `critique` (a scored UX review that ranks what to fix first), `audit` (accessibility, performance, theming, responsive, findings ranked P0 to P3), `harden` (overflow, long text, empty and error states: the same ground as `break-ui`), `clarify` (rewrites unclear copy: our "text says what it is"), `layout` and `typeset` (spacing, hierarchy, sizes), `distill` (removes what a surface does not need), and the detector, which can scan a rendered page: `npx impeccable detect http://localhost:<port>` finds low contrast, tiny text, nested cards and gray-on-colour text on the running product. **Do not use** `bolder`, `delight`, `overdrive` or `colorize`: they push toward louder, decorated pages, against our quiet doctrine. **Cautions:** its installer writes folders for many agents into the project and can add edit hooks, and `init` writes `PRODUCT.md`; run it from a scratch worktree or user-level install and never commit what it writes (our context files are VISION, AGENTS and the brief; `document` can capture the token sheet as `DESIGN.md` once UI-8's foundations exist, and only with Yash's yes); its `live` mode hot-swaps variants into source, so use it only on a throwaway branch. Running `npx` downloads a package, so ask Yash first. Its findings are advice filtered through our doctrine, like the HIG skill's.
- **[Taste Skill](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/taste-skill)** (MIT, about 106,000 stars, pushed 30 Oct): `design-taste-frontend`, a generator that gives an agent an art direction, real pictures from open archives and a browser self-check (three widths, up to three rounds of fixing its own screenshots). It is built for marketing pages and award-style motion, which our doctrine rejects for the product. **Use for:** (1) its dashboard and app-screen route as a **reference for our rules** (quiet panels, aligned figures, colour spent on data, equal margins, a clear title on the first screen, no random blank stretches), which are the same as what the brief asks of every surface; (2) its self-check loop as a **method** (screenshot at several widths, look, fix the top problems, repeat); (3) the **launch page and the product videos** (REL-4 and VIDEO-1), where a generated, art-directed page is the point. Not for the product's own UI. Finding pictures needs network access, so keep it away from private material.

A cheap first step at the start of AUDIT-1, with Yash's yes to the download: run Impeccable's detector on the running app for a few surfaces and record the findings as audit input, then judge on those results how much weight the two sets get, as with the HIG pilot.

A pilot comes first: run steps 2 to 4 on one surface (UI-16, the Files rail) and compare the result with what the installed skills alone give, before deciding how much weight the HIG skill gets.

**Window to 18 November (Yash, 29 Oct).** Speed to market matters, because anyone can build something like this and we want to be first, and the premium bar still holds. The scope for this window runs **as far as VIZ-1's ideas**, not necessarily their implementation:

1. **Complete P2 first.** The priority is smooth, premium current things: the P2 table (the audit, the UI passes, the motion passes and the rest) before anything new.
2. **Claude Code and Codex stay the supported harnesses.** Cursor and Antigravity (ADAPT-1) come only if time remains before 18 November, built from the sessions on this machine.
3. **VIZ-1:** its suggestions are thought through and started; if finished, good, otherwise it ships in the next update together with more agent support.
4. **MAC-1** (macOS acceptance) is a separate task so it is not forgotten; **VIDEO-1** (product videos) comes later.

The launch date is decided at the 18 November review, from where the list stands; no date is promised before it. Market advice recorded 29 Oct: Tuesday to Thursday, not Diwali week (8 November) and not US Thanksgiving week (26 November); the evidence for any month is anecdotal.

**Desktop only (Yash, 26 Oct).** Drishya runs on a laptop or PC against local sessions. Do not design, fix, measure or write tests for phone widths, landscape phones or touch input. The narrowest supported window is 600 px (a half-width laptop window); below it nothing is promised, and no item may be opened for it. Desktop-only also means no cloud, remote or multi-device surface.

**Real sessions, not only the demo (Yash, 26 Oct).** A change that affects what a card, reader, header, list or projection shows is checked on real local sessions of both harnesses that contain the case, as well as the fixture; record session id prefixes, sizes and counts only, keep screenshots in `.local/`, and say what could not be checked. The full rule is in AGENTS.md.

**Final passes wait (Yash, 26 Oct).** MOTION-1, MOTION-3 and UI-8 (the single UI pass; it absorbs UI-3, UI-4b and UI-7) start only when every feature item above them is done, and each is a whole pass over the finished product: look at what the content is by then, and only then decide what to change. Do not start, claim or schedule them earlier (MOTION-1 and UI-4b were started too early on 26 Oct and stopped).

**Lanes (26 Oct).** Three agents work this list at once, so each takes from its own lane first; a claim still decides, and `node scripts/claims.ts` is the first command of a session.

- **Harsh:** the reading-position jump, then DATA-3 and HOME-1 once he confirms the 26 Oct decision. PERF-2 is a final pass; REL-2 physical runs.
- **Claude (Yash's sessions):** READ-4/5/8, GIT-2, UI-1 once Yash has chosen the header, and the final UI passes. Verifies and merges its own PRs.
- **Codex (`yashdoke7`):** acceptance of the CONTENT-1…4 and CONTENT-7 readers, FILES-3/4/5, DATA-7/8/9 research, REVIEW-4. These need real samples or a native account, so they sit last in P1 rather than blocking the rows above them.
- **26 October hold lifted:** both builders confirmed deferring PERF-2/3; feature work in History/common/ingest/worker may proceed while preserving measured budgets (STATUS decisions). Active claims still apply: DATA-3 owns the turn ledger integration in `History.tsx`; coordinate that file before editing it. PERF-2/3 themselves remain deferred.

## 1. Priority order

**Overall: 22 of 100 done.** Marks are generated by `node scripts/sync-tasks.ts` from each item's checkbox below; tick the item, then run it.

### P0 — Release 1: a finished, packaged product (ship by 18 November)

**16 of 43 done**

Rebuilt by Yash with Claude, 8 November. Drishya is agent visualization: it presents what the agent is doing, faithfully and beautifully, at runtime. Release 1 ships Modern only, and its polish lands in Modern. Feature freeze at the end of 13 November; after that, only fixes from SHIP-10 and the gate. ## 2. Status snapshot

- **Workspace editing:** Workspace → select file → **Edit file** → **Compare draft** → **Review edit with harness**. Drafts are memory-only; disk changes only when the user submits a request and the native harness applies it. There is no direct save-to-disk.
- **Works today:** live capture and history; real diffs, outputs and test bars; file peek; Workspace drafts; Git review; Git branch atlas; cross-session search; Review work; Ctrl+K; seven palettes; native controller (local fixtures).
- **Measured on a 1,100-operation Codex session:** 2.3 s load, about 2.4k DOM nodes, 15 MB heap, about 7% idle main-thread time. Live latency is about 2 s.
- **Biggest gaps:** no "now"; no turn chapters; narrow wrapped diffs; half the harness data unused ([123](review/123-harness-data-audit.md)); reasoning mostly unrecorded by the harnesses themselves; the Git graph is art without information.

---

## 3. Detail by category

### SHIP — Release 1 (8 to 18 November)

Decided by Yash with Claude, 8 November: [release-1-scope.md](review/release-1-scope.md). Release 1 ships Modern; Release 1 polish lands in Modern; feature freeze at the end of 13 November. Accept every item on real sessions of both harnesses at 600 and 1440 px unless it says otherwise. Claim first; one builder per file at a time (SHIP-4, SHIP-5, SHIP-7 and SHIP-12 share `History.tsx`/`SessionWorkspace.tsx`; SHIP-2, SHIP-8 and MOTION-4 share stylesheets).

- [x] **SHIP-1 · Modern only** · P0 · `ui` `rel` — Yash, 8 Nov: the UI-version choice is unfinished and must not reach users.
  - **Do:** remove the UI-versions tab and its developer switch from Settings, and remove every route by which Modern, Legacy or Clarity can be selected in the product (URL parameter, stored preference, keyboard). A stored non-Modern preference falls back to Modern silently. The packaged build loads only Modern; Clarity's sources stay in the repository, unreachable from the product. Remove or update the specs and the clarity-guard capture that select versions; keep Modern's pixels unchanged.
  - **Accept:** a fresh package and an existing home with a stored Clarity or Legacy preference both open in Modern with no version wording anywhere in Settings or menus; a search of the built UI bundle finds no version chooser strings; `package:verify` passes.
  - **Checked 8 Nov, PR #369 (other-builder review pending):** packaged fresh/existing scratch homes and old preferences open only Modern, with five Settings sections; both genuine harnesses at 600/1440 px; four final history frames have zero changed pixels against main; package verification and related types/45 units pass. Version/controller/reading test migrations are complete. The broad browser snapshot is red; three Modern motion expectations reproduce on pre-change production code and are recorded for Claude/TEST-2 in [the verification record](review/ship-1-modern-only.md#claude-investigation). No all-green release gate is claimed.

  - **Accept:** on a real repository at 600 and 1440, switching among five tabs in different folders (including a nested one, a root file and a second worktree's file) always shows the right row highlighted and visible; a browser spec covers the expand, highlight and scroll.

The main repository's folders show only files evidenced in the main checkout. A nested worktree's folder is not browsable as ordinary repository content.

This comes only through the user's own installed and signed-in `gh` CLI, only after an explicit opt-in in Settings (Drishya sends nothing anywhere by itself, holds no token, and never fetches arbitrary URLs). - [x] **SHIP-14 · Queued inputs shows only the queue** · P0 · `live` `controller` `ui` — Yash, 8 Nov, with a screenshot: the dialog lists every enqueue receipt of the session (137), each with "item outcome unknown", so it reads as a backlog.
  - **Do:** the list shows only inputs that are still waiting. A receipt leaves the list when its prompt appears in History, a dequeue or removal is recorded, or the session has since gone idle (a later completed turn). Never claim it was delivered unless the matching prompt is in History. Receipts whose outcome cannot be established stay reachable under the existing Earlier receipts disclosure, labelled as history. When nothing is queued, the entry point does not show a count.

Units cover the resolution rules. **Complete for review:** genuine acceptance passes; full-gate failures reproduce on clean main. [Evidence and limits](review/ship-14-pending-queue.md).

### BUG — defects
- [ ] **CONTENT-19 · Blob-backed diff lines do not crash the reader** · P0 · `content` `bug` — found during SHIP-14 genuine acceptance, 9 Nov. Claude `f49a9e6d` (3,370,797 bytes) has two sealed blob references among 1,766 hunk lines. At replay step 467, DiffView in `apps/ui/src/components/common.tsx` calls `.slice(1)` on a non-string line and the view falls into ScreenBoundary. Reproduced at 600 px on both the release integration and rebuilt clean main `f93d880b`; no queue or hunk content was printed or changed.
  - **Do:** preserve exact sealed diff material/provenance while loading or honestly representing blob-backed lines before string-only diff rendering. Check the other hunk consumers too; never stringify a blob reference, drop a line, weaken redaction or fabricate source text. Claim before implementing.
  - **Accept:** genuine long-line diff at 600/1440 in History/replay remains readable and selectable with no renderer crash or lost bytes; a sealed long-line fixture guards each affected diff consumer. Private evidence: release integration `.local/release-real/`, clean-main `.local/release1-native-crash/`.

- [x] **BUG-1 · Wheel scroll trapped over history cards** · P0 · `bug` `read` — **fixed 19 Oct.** Cause: `overscroll-behavior: contain` on `.op-body`/`.result-area` (`ink.css`) blocked native chaining to `.history` at a card's edge. Vertical is now `auto` (horizontal stays contained). Verified on Codex and Claude sessions: at a card's top edge the wheel scrolls the history 300 px per step; mid-card scrolling stays local with the history unchanged.
  - **Problem:** with the pointer over a centred card, the wheel does not scroll the history, even when the card's own content cannot scroll further. Measured on the AgentGuard Claude session: `.history.scrollTop` stayed at 71627 for two wheel events over a Read card. Cards cover most of the centre, so this happens constantly.
  - **Do:** keep local result scrolling (constraint 7). At the inner scroll boundary, chain the remaining delta to `.history`; if the card has no overflow, never capture the wheel. Check the hover-reading and zoom handlers in `lib/hover-reading.ts` and `modern/local-scroll.ts` for `preventDefault` on wheel.
  - **Accept:** wheel over any card at its top or bottom boundary scrolls history; mid-content still scrolls locally; Follow detaches only on main-history scroll. Check Codex and Claude sessions with a trackpad and a mouse wheel.

- [x] **BUG-2 · Git review drawer covers the Git graph button; Escape inconsistent** · P0 · `bug` `git` `ui` — **fixed 19 Oct.** The right-docked Git drawer now starts below the measured navigation (`--drawer-top`, `ReadingWindow.tsx`), so Review ⇄ Graph is one click. Escape closes the newest open modeless reader from anywhere (menus close first; typing fields and modal dialogs are left alone). Verified on the Codex session at 1440 px: drawer top 187 px vs nav bottom 175 px; Review → Graph one click; Esc closes graph; menu then drawer in two presses.
  - **Problem:** with Git review open, its drawer overlays the Git graph button, so you cannot switch Review → Graph without closing first. Escape closes Settings and Ctrl+K but not Git review or the ⋯ session menu.
  - **Do:** make Review ⇄ Graph a tab switch inside the same Git surface, or keep the right navigation above the drawer. Escape closes the top-most transient surface (menu → drawer → reader) and returns focus to its opener.
  - **Accept:** from either Git surface, one click reaches the other; Escape order verified for menu, Git review, Git graph, peek and Settings; focus returns to the trigger.

Links stay display-only (`md-link`); opening local paths in peek remains FILES-2. Three unit tests (`apps/ui/test/markdown-links.test.ts`) and the real Codex message verified.

Measured 0 overlaps at 640/768/820/853/900/1024/1100 px (853 and 1024 stand in for 150% and 125% zoom of a 1280 window); unchanged at 1280+ and ≤ 600 px. **Residual (moved to UI-1):** the small bottom-corner pills (collapsed Tools, Follow live) still float over the edge of prose at narrow widths, and the phone layout was not re-audited. - [x] **BUG-6 · Usage shows "Not reported" for Codex although `token_count` is recorded** · `bug` `data` — fixed by DATA-2 in PR149, pending review.

- [x] **BUG-7 · Live refresh during session open drops the restored reader** · P1 · `bug` `live` — **fixed 19 Oct.** `reloadProjection` now waits for an in-flight open of the same recording before taking its token; the open settles once its location is applied, so its own live-start reload cannot wait on itself. New `open-refresh-race.test.ts` failed before and passes; "session detours" passes 10/10 at 150 ms and 10/10 with coalescing forced to 25 ms.
  - **Problem:** `openRecording` (`apps/ui/src/state.ts`) clears the reading location, awaits recordings and projection, then applies the saved location. A live refresh that starts meanwhile (`useLiveUpdates` → `reloadProjection`) takes the newest projection token, so `openRecording` discards its result and the saved reader is never restored. Reproduced every time with 25 ms coalescing; PR128 restored 150 ms, which only makes it unlikely.
  - **Accept:** a unit test reproduces it; `fresh-workspace.spec.ts` "session detours" passes 10/10 alone, also with coalescing temporarily at 25 ms.
- [x] **BUG-8 · Fake file paths from search output** · P1 · `bug` `read` — Yash, 27 Oct: the Files tree shows folders that exist nowhere (`ok 146 tests`, `1) tests`, `[270`, `0-trace.stacks`). **Cause:** `parseHitLines` (`packages/projection/src/shell.ts`) takes everything before `:NN:` as a path, so a `grep` over Playwright output (`ok 146 tests/browser/x.spec.ts:12:1 › title`, `[270/408] tests/browser/…`, `1) tests/…`) registers fake files as search hits; they reach the Files tree, per-file counts, the file timeline and the link graph (`file:<fake path>`), which breaks "never invent a location". **Do:** accept a text-parsed hit only if it is a plausible path (no whitespace in a relative path; an absolute path may contain spaces), a unit test with the exact junk lines, bump the reducer so old recordings re-project, and report counts of fake paths before and after on real local sessions. **Accept:** none of those folders appear for a session that ran such greps; real hits are unchanged.
  - **27 Oct, fixed (reducer r18):** a text-parsed search hit becomes a file reference only if `plausibleHitPath` accepts it (relative paths may not contain whitespace; absolute paths may). The hit itself stays in the search card as captured text; only the file registration, per-file counts, file timeline and link graph change. **Real sessions (counts only):** a static 83 MB Claude session: search-hit paths 215 → 184 (exactly the 31 relative paths with whitespace, 19 distinct, 13 fake top-level folders), real hits unchanged; a live 195 MB Claude session: fake top-level folders 24 → 0, whitespace paths 56 → 0. Unit tests use the exact junk lines. **Known limit:** a real relative file name containing a space is no longer decorated in the tree when it appears only in grep text (it stays in the card); structured search results (`files` lists) are unaffected. Old recordings re-project on the reducer bump. DATA-3 (#206) also sets r18 and takes r19 when it merges.

A status dot colours it (green running, amber asking, indigo working). The tooltip gives the exact basis ("A call is open: the harness recorded it … and no result yet", "This turn so far: 7 operations."). The tab title becomes `● Running · <repo>`, `? Needs you · <repo>` or `◐ Working · <repo>` and the favicon gets a matching dot only while the agent is active; both restore on reply, quiet or leaving the session. No line at all for a session whose newest event is over two hours old. New `GET /v1/recordings/:id/now` (via the history worker, no schema change) returns facts from the **tail** of the recording, so the line stays right while the person is reading older history. Pure logic with an injected clock: `packages/scene/src/now.ts` and `apps/ui/src/lib/now-state.ts` (21 tests). Verified on this very conversation's live Claude session (running → working → running as my own calls opened and closed), on an old session (no line), Home (title and favicon restored), and the expanded composer.

### TURN — turns and navigation

- [x] **TURN-1 · Turn chapters with ledger** · P0 · `turn` — **shipped 19 Oct (header and ledger; collapse split out as TURN-1b).** Each prompt is a chapter: its words clamped to three lines with "Show full prompt" only when it actually overflows, and, once the turn is over, one quiet mono line of what it did, e.g. `edited 28 files (+374 −46) · 31 commands · 18 m 18 s` (parts shown only when non-zero: read, searched, edited files with +/−, commands, tests `41✓ 1✗`, web, MCP, delegated, `N failed` in red). The tooltip says it is counted from captured operations and that the duration runs to the last captured time in the turn (derived; not every harness records where a turn ends). The newest turn shows no ledger while the agent is active (the Now line is live), and replay shows none, so nothing tells the future. Counts come from a new `GET /v1/recordings/:id/turns` (whole recording, so paged sessions are right; small sessions are counted in the browser) built on the pure `packages/scene/src/turns.ts`; passages carry `turnStep` to find their ledger (goldens updated: only the new field). Verified on the 41-turn Codex session (28-file, 18-minute turn) and this live session; clamp 123 px ↔ 345 px expanded. Tests: scene 6, ledger wording 5, daemon route 1.
  - **No schema change.** Codex's recorded `duration_ms` (review 125 S1) would replace the derived duration later. Tests inside Codex `exec` wrappers show as `0` runs until DATA-1.

- [x] **TURN-1b · Collapse finished turns** · P1 · `turn` `motion` — **shipped 20 Oct (Modern only).** The turn's ledger line is the fold control (a chevron turns; no new chrome). A folded turn keeps its prompt and ledger; the rest of its passages become zero-height slots. The composer now marks every passage with `inTurn` (goldens: only the new field), so paged-out members fold too and a paged-out opener still shows as a header. Folds are stored by the opening passage's id (stable while live work arrives) in working locations (`history/folded-turns`, persisted across reloads); the open height is kept separately from the folded one, so unfolding lands where it was. The header never moves: the reading anchor holds, and when folding near the end would shorten the document under the reader, the old extent is held and shrinks as they scroll back. The selected/expanded operation's turn and any prompt-rail jump unfold it (and it stays open); the newest turn cannot fold while the agent is active; replay never folds. Motion: work below the header travels from where it was (260 ms), unfolded work eases in (220 ms); finishes on wheel, off for reduced motion/low resource. Verified on the 38-turn Codex session (header moved 0 px folding and unfolding; 18 → 10 cards on screen). Tests: `apps/ui/test/turn-fold.test.ts` 3, scene membership 1, `tests/browser/turn-fold.spec.ts` 3 (the clamp case failed before the extent hold). Bulk fold/unfold keys shipped with TURN-2 (`c` folds other finished turns, `C` unfolds all); the ten-minute live run is still a MOTION-3 check.
  - Original brief: the history is a custom virtual list (`History.tsx`: measured passage heights, run-based layout, scroll anchors, paged windows with unloaded placeholders), and only the passage that opens a turn knows its turn. Collapsing must keep: scroll anchoring when a header above the viewport collapses; prompt-rail and search jumps into a collapsed turn (auto-expand); selection/expanded operations inside it; Follow and the newest turn (never collapsible while the agent is active); windows that start mid-turn (membership unknown); persisted height estimates. Suggested shape: turn membership computed in the scene composer (as `turnStep` was), collapse state in workspace memory by `turnStep`, collapsed passages rendered as zero-height slots with the header and ledger still visible, plus golden and manual-scroll tests. Accept: collapse/expand never moves the reading position, every jump target auto-expands, ten-minute live run stays calm.

- [x] **TURN-2 · Keyboard reading model** · P1 · `turn`
  - **Do:** keys:
    - `j`/`k` next/previous operation (down/up); `n`/`p` next/previous edit; `f` next failure; `t`/`T` next/previous turn;
    - `e` expand/collapse; `o` open in peek; `y` copy the focused material; `g`/`G` first/last;
    - `?` cheat sheet.

    The focused operation gets a visible focus ring and scrolls into the reading position. Keys are disabled while typing in inputs or the editor.
  - **Accept:** all keys work with Follow on and off; the cheat sheet is listed in Settings → Keyboard.
  - **20 October implementation:** Modern history uses a quiet real-card focus ring, without selecting/opening the inspector. Bounded worker cursors navigate the whole recording (not only resident pages), honor operation filters, and use captured turn identity across revisions. `c` folds other finished turns while preserving the reading anchor; `C` unfolds all, completing TURN-1b's deferred bulk shortcut. `o` uses the existing peek/consent route; `y` copies a selection or the material's existing copy action, otherwise the card preview (never claims to copy an unavailable full artifact). `?` and Settings → Keyboard open the same guide, including from Home. The arrows people see come from `FlowConnections` (inside `History.tsx`) and showed no card crossings. No code changed. READ-4 stays open for the real refinements (passage layout, keep wrap plus arrows). **UI-4 candidate:** `fresh/FreshApp.tsx` and `modern/ConnectedPassage.tsx` look like dead code from the older independent frontend draft; other Modern components import small pieces from `fresh/` (for example `CodeTokens`), so delete only the unreferenced files, after checking each.
  - **26 October, measured and guarded (still open, two items need a decision):** `tests/browser/passage-layout.spec.ts` (12 tests: the checkout-fix and Codex fixtures plus a 12-tool synthetic passage with two wide diffs, a long command title, a source card and tiny cards after a full row; 1024/1280/1440/1920 px; viewport 6000 px tall so every wire is drawn) pins what already holds: reading order is row-major in every passage; no wrapped row starts with a card that fitted on the row above (no orphan); every consecutive pair has exactly one wire that leaves the previous card's edge, enters the next card's edge, runs right edge to left edge between neighbours on a row, and crosses no card (0 crossings and 0 missing wires over 38 cards and 28 wires at each of the four widths); and the row structure of all three recordings. Shown to fail when forced: `row-reverse` and a reordered card fail the order check, a wider gap fails the golden rows, and disabling the obstruction test in `flow-route.ts` fails with wires crossing cards. Layout is plain CSS wrap (`.op.shape-*` flex-basis in `ink.css`/`stage.css`, greedy `flex-wrap` in `p1.css`), so an orphan cannot occur; wires come from `FlowConnections` + `lib/flow-route.ts` (an obstructed route is omitted, never painted over a card). **Still open, both are visible changes so they wait for a builder decision:** (1) *wide diff cards do not start a row*: with the 12-tool passage at 1920 px a 663 px Edit card sits between two 210 px cards (`compact · wide · compact`); at 1024-1440 px it starts its own row only because nothing fits beside it. Enforcing the rule needs a forced break before `.op-edit.shape-wide` (the card kind and shape are already classes), a different composition at wide widths. (2) *stacked cards exit by the left edge*: when every card is on its own row (4-card passages at 1024 px, and any passage whose cards are wider than half the row) `flowRoute` tries the left-gutter route before the vertical gap route, so a wire leaves the left edge of the previous card, drops down a gutter 12 px outside the leftmost visible card and enters the next card's left edge; contiguous pairs then read as one long bracket. Sending the wire from the previous card's bottom edge to the next card's top edge would match the contract's wording. No code changed in this slice (the routing lives in non-held `lib/flow-route.ts`; the wrap is CSS), and no real private session was opened: acceptance on three real sessions is a builder-run check using the same spec's measurements.

### EXPLAIN — IDEA-6, a parked idea (not planned)

**Not being done (Yash, 26 Oct):** kept as one idea, not as work. The notes below are design notes only, with no subtask checkboxes and no implementation claims; the unchecked IDEA-6 umbrella above records its parked status only. Nothing here is built or scheduled.

**Deferred by the builder:** this depends on an external service (Token Factory key, model ids, cost and latency we do not control). The product is built and finished without it; these items come back only after every item above is done. The code that exists (`packages/explain`, the opt-in briefing and explain routes) stays as it is, off unless `NEBIUS_API_KEY` is set, and nothing new is built on it.

Rules: opt-in per call or per session; preview the exact payload; content redacted at ingest and again before sending; output labelled "Generated by <model>" in a distinct derived style; every claim cites captured evidence IDs (`E1…En`); uncited claims are dropped and counted. This fills the *why* that harnesses no longer record ([123](review/123-harness-data-audit.md) §1). It never replaces captured text and is never presented as the agent's thinking.

- **EXPLAIN-4 · Live validation** · P3 · `explain` — get a Token Factory key; list models (`GET /v1/models`); set real Nano/Super/Ultra ids (defaults in `packages/explain/src/index.ts` are unverified); run explain, briefing and labels on a real session; record latency and cost per call in a short note under docs/review.

- **EXPLAIN-1 · Narrator labels** · P3 · `explain` `live`
  - **Do:**
    - With the narrator on for a session, each burst of consecutive operations (or each passage) gets a one-line generated intent above it, e.g. "Investigating why checkout rejects free orders [E3,E5]", from Nano.
    - Batched, debounced (≥ 10 s or end of passage), cached by evidence hash, cancellable.
    - Shown in the derived style with the model name on hover.
    - The narrator off means zero network calls.
  - **Accept:** works live on a Codex and a Claude session; no call before opt-in; labels cite real operations; cost and latency within the targets recorded in EXPLAIN-4.

- **EXPLAIN-2 · Turn summary and return briefing** · P3 · `explain` `turn`
  - **Do:** on request (button in the turn ledger, or "While you were away" after the tab was hidden for more than 2 min), Super writes 3–5 cited bullets: what changed, what was checked, what failed, and what is still open. Each bullet links to its evidence.

- **EXPLAIN-3 · Explain this hunk (validate)** · P3 · `explain` — the existing Ultra/Super path; validate on real diffs; citations jump to the step.

- **EXPLAIN-5 · Derived visual language** · P3 · `explain` `ui` — one consistent treatment for generated text (for example an italic secondary tint, a small model glyph and a dotted underline on citations) that can never be mistaken for agent prose or captured output, including in exports.

### GIT — Git review, graph and Sacred Timeline

- [x] **GIT-1 · Sacred Timeline** · P1 · `git` `motion` — **finished 20 Oct at the builder's request** (no longer a prototype).
  - **20 October, finished:** branches are grouped into real runs (first-parent walk), each on a seeded side and the nearest free tier, just outside the river's rim (or outside the branch it left), with a slow seeded drift; the river meanders and fades in from older history and out past the newest commit. A strand starts thin and gains width at every commit (new filaments fade in at that commit), never past half the river. Space is black with white stars. Hovering a strand between commits names the branch (its ref), whether and where it merged, where it left, authors, time span and its commits; clicking it opens the newest commit; a commit peek says which commit of its branch it is. Zoom keeps the point under the pointer (buttons: the middle) in the same commit as the new scale, with room added at either end when needed; pinch and Ctrl/⌘ + wheel zoom the graph instead of the page; `+`/`−` keys; buttons step ×1.4 and disable at the limits. Labels of branches below the river sit below it. Tests: `git-light.test.ts` 8; Git atlas spec checks Ctrl + wheel anchoring (< 12 px) and the branch peek. **Not measured:** 60 fps with 500 commits on the reference machine (PERF-1/REL-2); the octopus/shallow/detached matrix is covered by the unchanged topology layout's tests, not re-run visually.
  - **20 October, builder follow-up:** (1) Zoom now magnifies: the river, branch distances and lightning scale with zoom (river 56 px × scale, 14–120 px), where before only the commit spacing grew, so "zoom in" looked like a sideways stretch. (2) Squash merges are shown merging: Git keeps no parent link for a squash, so the daemon now returns content signatures (`git-history.ts` `squashEvidence`: sorted path + written blob, hashed) for each non-merge commit's own change and for each branch head's whole change since it left the primary line; the UI joins them across loaded pages, and a branch whose change a newer primary commit wrote exactly flows into that commit to the right ("squash-merged into main at …" in its peek, with the commit named). No match, no line. On this repository 42 of 65 graph-unmerged branches match. Forks rise early and merges drop late, so a branch travels beside the river and flows in at its merge. Squash-merged heads get no lightning. Tests: daemon squash evidence 1, `git-light.test.ts` 10.
  - **20 October progress:** step 1 prototype landed as [`docs/prototype/sacred-timeline.html`](prototype/sacred-timeline.html) (synthetic topology; WebGL2 additive filaments, half-float bloom, starfield, DOM commits/refs/peek/keyboard; variants River, Ice, Storm; `?lite=1`). Builder looked at the stills and asked to integrate directly and keep iterating toward the reference.
  - **20 October, integrated:** `GitThreadGraph` now draws the light (`lib/git-light.ts` geometry, `lib/light-renderer.ts` WebGL2) under the unchanged DOM commits, labels, peek, paging and keyboard; the SVG ribbon and Orbit remain only without WebGL2. Screen-space and view-bounded: redraws on pan/zoom/hover only (idle 0), ≤ 40k segments (8k and no bloom in low resource). Real-repo fixes: branch commits keep a screen-space clearance from the river at every zoom (buttons use the same positions); lightning size follows the branch's loaded width and only opens forward (builder: flow must read left → right); white-hot core with magenta/violet rims. Verified on this repository (82 refs, squash-merge history). Tests: `git-light.test.ts` 5, Git atlas spec updated for the lit path. **Remaining:** 60 fps measurement with 500 commits, the fork/merge acceptance matrix (octopus, shallow, detached), optional flow motion; the row list is GIT-3. Squash-merged PRs leave no merge edge in Git, so those branches end at their head; GIT-2 is where a session/PR link could show that.

- [x] **GIT-6 · The Git graph: fast, quieter controls, and branch focus** · P1 · `git` `ui` `perf` — builder request, 31 October: the commit graph keeps its look (the Sacred Timeline texture and colours, black space with white stars) but lags a lot and should be done better and lighter on resources; the buttons around it are not good; and different things can be built around it, starting with selecting a branch and having that branch highlighted.
  - **Measured (31 Oct, Claude, this repository, 50 commits loaded, 1440 px, headless Edge):** the GPU draw is 0.3 ms, but the JavaScript that rebuilds the light geometry runs on every redraw (every pan frame, every pointer move over the graph, every frame of the hover fade): 14 to 20 ms for 52,960 line segments (above the 40,000 the code means to cap at), with spikes of 38 to 51 ms while panning, zooming and hovering. A slower laptop multiplies that. The geometry is rebuilt because it is generated in screen space for the current view.
  - **Do:** (1) **Performance:** build the line geometry once in world coordinates for a tile around the view and reuse it while the view moves (the GPU shifts it); rebuild only when the view leaves the tile, the scale changes or the data changes; make hover and focus a cheap brightness change on the cached geometry, not a rebuild; make the geometry loop itself allocation-free; upload to the GPU only when the data changes; keep the segment cap true. Same picture, measured before and after. (2) **Controls:** the controls around the graph (zoom, fit, orbit, the commit chooser and whatever else is there) become one quiet, aligned group in the product's own style, with nothing that does not earn its place. (3) **Branch focus:** choosing a branch (a strand, a branch in the ref list, or a ref filter) highlights it and dims the rest, shows its fork and merge points, and steps along its commits with previous and next; Escape clears it; the selection survives paging. Topology stays truthful: no invented branches or links.
  - [x] **31 October, complete (Claude):** (1) **Performance:** the light geometry is built once in world coordinates for a tile three viewports wide and reused while the view stays inside it (the GPU shifts it with an offset uniform); it is rebuilt only when the scale or the data changes or the view leaves the tile; hover and focus are a cheap brightness pass on the cached lines (no rebuild); the geometry loop writes into one growing typed array with no per-segment allocation; the renderer uploads lines only when their key changes. Measured on this repository (50 commits loaded, 73,696 segments, 1440 px, headless Edge) before to after: panning 14 to 20 ms of geometry work per frame to 0.1 ms with 0 rebuilds and a worst frame of 49 ms to 5 ms; hover sweep 13.7 ms to 0.0 ms, worst frame 38 ms to 6 ms; zoom steps 18.5 ms to 5.7 ms per rebuild, worst frame 34 ms to 13 ms; drag pan worst 33 ms to 5 ms; frames over 33 ms went from 5 across the four runs to 0. The look is unchanged (same geometry, colours, stars and lightning). (2) **Controls:** zoom, level and fit are one hairline group of icon buttons; the commit chooser and the status line are one slim row at the bottom; the river is centred vertically after Fit instead of sitting low and clipped. (3) **Branch focus:** clicking a strand (or B on a commit) focuses its branch and dims the rest; a chip shows the branch, its place ("2 of 5") with previous and next along its commits, and clear; Escape clears it without closing the dialog; hovering a ref in the list previews its branch; the focused branch's peek offers Open newest commit; stepping selects without leaving the graph. Tests: `git-graph-focus.spec.ts` (2, at 1440 and 700 px: controls, pan without rebuild, focus, step, clear, Escape), affected run (types, units, browsers) green. Not measured: a repository with 500+ loaded commits (this repository has 348 refs and 50 commits loaded per page); low-resource mode was not re-measured (its cap is unchanged and its units pass); not checked on a Codex session's repository (the graph reads the local Git, which both harnesses share).
  - **Accept:** frame time while panning, zooming and hovering at 1440 px on this repository and on a repository with 500+ loaded commits is measured before and after, with the worst frame and the number over 33 ms recorded; the geometry rebuild no longer happens per pan frame; the look is unchanged at rest (before and after screenshots at 600 and 1440 px); low-resource mode still renders; real repositories of both harness sessions (this one and a Codex session's) show branch focus correctly, including a squash-merged branch; keyboard reaches every control; the Git atlas, branches and review specs pass.

Direction: all types of content are shown immediately when observed.

Small/large native text rows show without a later event (individual 816–2,662 ms measurements, not p95). [Full evidence and limits](review/live-5-completion.md). Text absent from a native journal is never invented.
  - **Found (31 Oct, Claude, counts only, on this session's own journal `dd136b25`):** a message typed while the agent is working is not a `user` row. Claude Code records it as a `queue-operation` (`enqueue`, with the text) and delivers it into the turn as an `attachment` of type `queued_command` (`prompt` is the text or content blocks with images; `commandMode` is `prompt` for a person's message and `task-notification` for a background task's notice). The Claude adapter ignored that attachment type, so none of them reached the projection: 13 mid-turn messages and 23 task notices in this one journal (8 images among the block-form prompts) were missing, none duplicated as a `user` row. `queue-operation` rows whose message was removed before delivery (2 of 66 enqueues here) have no delivery and are not shown. Fixed in the adapter (below).
  - **Text that arrives late, not reproduced on small sessions:** a real Claude Code run on a throwaway repository (a text, a 20-second foreground command, a text) wrote the first text row 0.8 s before the tool call and 21 s before its result, so the harness does not hold text back until a tool finishes; replaying those real rows into a live recording shows each text within 0.2 to 0.35 s with nothing after it; a synthetic live append (text alone, text after a finished tool, final text) shows within 0.35 s. The large-recording measurement is below.
  - **31 October, part 1 done (adapter):** `queued_command` attachments are now captured as the person's message (text and images, dated when queued); background task notices arrive the same way and are recognised by the existing injected-message reader. Units: 3 adapter tests (text, text and image blocks, empty and other attachments stay ignored); on a tiny journal the projection gains the turn and the live page holds the text (2 matches in the DOM, projection 2 turns). It still arrives at the harness's delivery time, which is the next tool boundary.
  - **Why a typed message still feels late (found 31 Oct):** Claude Code writes the `queue-operation` `enqueue` row with the text the moment it is typed, but only writes the delivering `queued_command` at the next tool boundary, so Drishya shows the message when a tool finishes. Showing it at `enqueue` as a queued message, then delivered when the attachment or the `user` row arrives, and withdrawn if a `remove` row follows (2 of 66 enqueues in this journal were removed undelivered), needs a small projection design (a queued state) and agreement; it is the next step of this item.
  - **Text that is never in the journal (found 31 Oct, live on this session):** visible assistant text written just before a tool call is sometimes absent from the journal altogether. A marker sentence written in a message before a 25-second tool call was not in the journal during the call, nor in the 10 minutes after; three more such texts are also absent, while others from the same session are present as `text` rows (for example the messages before `Merged. Now syncing both trees`). In the journal the missing ones show as `thinking` summaries only (25 messages here have the shape thinking, thinking-summary, tool_use with no text row). No other file under `~/.claude` or the app's folder holds them, and the request blobs hold no assistant text that lacks a row (59 of 59 are present as rows). Drishya cannot show text its source does not contain; this needs the harness behaviour confirmed (a Claude Code version question) before a fix is chosen. What is measured on Drishya's side: text rows show within 0.2 to 0.45 s on a small live recording, 0.75 to 1.6 s on a 30 MB copy of this session, with motion on or off, whether or not a tool call is still running.
  - **Do:** (1) capture `queued_command` as the person's message (text and images), dated when it was queued, with a background task's notice recognised by the existing injected-message reader; (2) find where a live text waits for a later event on a large real session, fix it, and add a regression check that appends text alone to a live recording and requires it on screen within a stated time; (3) check the Codex equivalent (messages sent while a turn is running) and record what Codex writes.

  - **Cause (read from the code, checked on this machine):** the indexer's walk (`packages/indexer/src/index.ts`, `SKIP_DIRS` plus `.gitignore`) skips `node_modules`, `.git` and the like but not a nested checkout. A Claude Code agent worktree lives inside the project at `.claude/worktrees/<name>/` and is a complete copy of the repository with its own `.git` file; this workspace has 12 of them. The walk descends into every copy, so the 5,000-file cap is reached by duplicates, the real files are cut off, and the map and tree show each file many times. FILES-6 fixed the tree for worktrees *recorded as separate roots* (Codex); it does not cover worktrees *inside* the workspace.

  - **Accept:** units for a `.git` file, a `.git` directory, a nested checkout under a hidden folder, the root's own `.git` (not a nested checkout) and the file cap not being consumed by a nested copy; on the real `drishya` workspace (12 agent worktrees) the file count, the `truncated` flag and the share of `.claude/worktrees` paths before and after (counts only); the map and the Files tree at 600 and 1440 px on a real Claude Code and a real Codex session of that workspace show only the project's own folders; the map status names the skipped checkouts.
  - **29 October, done:** the walk skips any directory below the root that has its own `.git` entry (file or directory); `isIndexableFile` applies the same rule; `INDEXER_VERSION` is `imports-rules-v2`; the map legend adds "N nested checkouts not shown" (`stats.nestedCheckouts`, optional). Real `drishya` workspace (12 agent worktrees), counts only: before, 5,000 files, 7,496 imports, truncated, 4,999 of the 5,000 paths under `.claude/worktrees`; after, 963 files, 1,498 imports, not truncated, 0 such paths, 12 nested checkouts reported. Real sessions with that workspace: Claude Code `rec_061e` (7,150,502 bytes) and Codex `rec_e77f` (10,412,190 bytes) show 964 map items and 12 nested checkouts at 1440 and 600 px (Claude, in the browser; Codex, map data), and the Files tree names no worktree. Eleven indexer units (four new), `tests/browser/nested-checkouts.spec.ts` at 1440x900 and 600x720, `check:affected` green (460 + 33 browser). **Not covered:** a nested checkout whose `.git` was removed would be indexed as ordinary files; Codex agent worktrees recorded as separate roots stay with FILES-6.

  - **What is recorded (counted on this machine, 29 Oct):** Claude Code: 7 sessions with direct `computer` calls (32 calls: 27 `screenshot`, 2 `wait`, 2 `scroll_to`, 1 `scroll`; 18 results carry a JPEG plus text), another 136 calls to `mcp__Claude_Browser__computer` across 28 sessions, and 349 more named inside batched calls in 4 sessions; actions carry `coordinate`, `ref`, `region`, `scale` and `tabId` when used. The recorded action is drawn on it in the screenshot's own coordinate frame, scaled by the image size read from the bytes and the recorded `scale`: a ring for click, double and right click, a line between captured start and end for a drag, the typed text or key chord as a label, scroll as direction and amount. A step that names an element or `ref` but no position shows its label and no marker; a position is never guessed.
    - **Motion:** moving to the next step is one real material change: the next captured screenshot replaces the frame (cross-fade with a short zoom toward the acted-on region), the marker appears once and holds. No cursor path between points (it is not recorded), no typing animation. Replay timing follows captured timestamps with long gaps capped and labelled; in sequence replay later frames are not revealed early.
    - **Strip and context:** a frame strip of the captured thumbnails scrubs the run (arrow keys, Home/End); each step shows its tab, URL or window title when captured, step n of N, and a link to its raw operation card. A step with no new screenshot keeps the last frame labelled "no new frame captured". A frame whose bytes were not kept shows its recorded size and "image not retained".

A different, replaced, redirected or missing checkout, another worktree, a conflicting identity or a redaction mismatch never restores it.
    2. Revoke and change through the API. The existing workspace revoke also clears the remembered grant, and pending reads are invalidated as today.
    3. A setup-state API for a selected session, which submits nothing and starts no turn. It reports: the session's exact root and identity; any existing grant; whether a sibling checkout of the same repository has a grant (the pre-fill signal); and whether the selected harness CLI is available, signed in and supported.
    4. The idle-other-clients acknowledgement is asked at the first send to a selected session and held until that connection ends: a reload, leaving the session, a controller disconnect or a failure. A model or effort change no longer resets it.

    - Revoking clears the grant, and the next open asks again.
    - A missing or replaced root and an identity mismatch do not restore the grant.
    - The first send asks for the acknowledgement and later sends in the same connection do not; a reload or reconnect asks again.
    - A missing, signed-out or unsupported CLI is reported without submitting anything.
    - Recorded-only viewing works throughout.

- [x] **DATA-12 · A continued Claude Code session shows its whole history, across the files it continues** · P2 · `data` `content` `home` — builder report, 31 October: "previously we could see the whole history, now it's just up to some particular point, maybe a Claude Code update; shouldn't they be present locally, so we get the whole history instead of just what Claude shows?"
  - **Found (Claude, 31 Oct, counts only):** the history is on disk. The current Drishya working session is journal `dd136b25` (19.7 MB, 29 to 31 Oct, 3 compactions). It does not start at the conversation's start: its first row after metadata is a `compact_boundary` whose `logicalParentUuid` is a message in the earlier journal `c757fa6a` (234.5 MB, 61,002 rows, 19 to 29 Oct), and 277 user/assistant rows (their uuids) appear in both files. Drishya imports each journal as its own recording, so it shows `dd136b25` from its first compaction onward (621 operations, which matches the 622 tool calls the file holds) and nothing of the earlier file; Claude Code's own view does the same. Drishya has no code that reads `logicalParentUuid` or compares uuids between files. Not verified: that this is a Claude Code update (the other 151 local journals were not checked for a split), or how many sessions are affected.
  - **Do:** (1) detect a continuation from the journal itself: a first `compact_boundary` whose `logicalParentUuid` (or whose shared uuids) is found in another local journal; the link is evidence, never inferred from time or folder. (2) Show the lineage as one history: the earlier file's history first, a quiet marker where the continuation begins ("continued from an earlier session file"), then this file, with the earlier part openable on its own. Never copy or rewrite either journal; shared rows are shown once; if the earlier file is gone (retention) say so rather than hide it. (3) Counts, rail, search, Git and replay treat the lineage as one recording or say clearly which part they cover. This is a store and projection question (a recording of several journals), so it needs both builders' agreement and an event-schema note before the change.
  - [x] **1 November, complete (Codex built it, Claude finished and merged):** a Claude journal whose first compaction names a parent message that exactly one other local journal owns is joined to it as one history (earlier file first, a quiet "Continued from an earlier session file" receipt where the continuation begins, with Open earlier part); the join is derived metadata (`session_lineage`, no new observation kind, no rewritten observation), identical shared parts appear once and a differing usage receipt is kept; a missing or ambiguous parent says so and stays independent; unrelated sessions are never linked; native controls, current Files and Git stay on the selected (latest) file. Real pair `c757fa6a` + `dd136b25`: 2 physical parts, 1 exact link, 277 shared message records, 392 identical parts omitted, 1 differing receipt retained, transition at the first retained continuation turn; the receipt shows at 600 and 1440 px (frames in `.local/`, not committed). Local scan of 153 journals: 13 start with a parent reference (5 unique, 5 ambiguous, 3 missing), recorded in `docs/review/data-12-session-lineage.md`. Single-file sessions unchanged (check:real on 3 of them: one part, no join). Units 9, browsers 7 (600/1440, live append, replay boundary, missing/ambiguous).
  - **Accept:** on the real pair above, the full history from 19 Oct to now reads as one lineage with the shared rows once; a continuation whose parent is missing says so; an unrelated session never gets linked; no change to a session that is a single file; live and replay agree; 600 and 1440 px. Check other local journals for the same split first and record the count.

- [ ] **AUDIT-1 · Feature-by-feature product audit before the UI passes** · P2 · `ui` `data` `content` `read` `git` `files` `review` — Yash, 29 October. Many features were added one at a time and some are badly presented or only half handled by the backend: information does not reach the user with ease of navigation, Changes has to be paged through one page at a time (page 256 of 256 on a large session), and the content of some views barely says what it is stating. Features exist; the output and its presentation are not yet a product.
  - **Default state (Yash, 30 Oct):** the audit also lists every surface that starts collapsed or minimized and whether its content should be shown by default (UI-17 is the first example: the plan strip). The tool list added by TOOLS-1 is shown by default too.
  - **Depth (Yash, 5 Nov) — the standard for every audit, UI and motion pass:** explore as a real user doing real work, far deeper than his own ten-minute review (which found eight items). Send prompts and attach screenshots; open every menu and popover; compare each surface with Claude Code, ChatGPT and Codex (chat layout, prompt capsule, composer, menus, tables, diagrams); ask of every fact whether a person would read it; check that every motion opens and closes the same way; try other screen sizes and browser zoom; look at every feature, component, div and pixel against the premium bar. His findings are the floor, not the scope. A pass is not done until it survives this kind of use.
  - **Depth (Yash, 30 Oct):** for every view that has a short form and an expanded form (hover card, row, peek, Inspector, dialog), the audit asks whether everything recorded is reachable (not "N more" with no way to open them), whether the expanded form carries more than the short one and in a shape a person can read (a list, not a block of the harness's raw text), and whether counts say what they count. CONTENT-13 (web search) is the first example found; every other result view is checked the same way, and the UI and motion passes carry the same depth.
  - **User's-eye audit (Yash, 31 Oct):** the audit is also done as a user who has just opened the product: do the files and the Workspace give a path to read the whole file the agent touched, and does every place that shows an edit, a write or a read offer it? A feature that exists but does not help the person (a dead end, an error where an explanation belongs, a view you cannot get out of toward the thing you wanted) is a finding as much as a missing one, and so is anything that would help and is not there. Friendliness is part of the audit, even where it adds a feature.
  - **Interaction inventory (Yash, 31 Oct):** for every component, every kind of interaction it accepts (click, double click, right click, hover, focus and Tab, keyboard, scroll and wheel, drag, Escape, selection and copy, resize), what the product does for each today, on real sessions, in live and replay, loading, empty and failing: written as a file the UI and motion passes use as their checklist. See "The whole-product passes, planned" above.
  - **Scope:** every function that is built today. Inventory first (header and session menu, Home, history cards by action type, prompt rail and turns, live and replay, Files, peek, Workspace, Git review, Git graph, Changes, Search, packets and bookmarks, Inspector, composer and controller, notifications, settings, repository map, and whatever the inventory finds that is not listed here). Yash's own list and any native sessions he supplies are the minimum; do not fit the pass to them.
  - **Method:** use each function as a developer would, on real sessions of both harnesses (small, large and very large), at 600 and 1440 px, live and replay. For each one record: its purpose; what a developer needs there; what is shown; and defects in four places: **UI** (space, layout, controls), **backend** (data not handled to the feature's need, missing or misleading values, slow paths, limits that hide things), **content** (does the text and shape say what it is, in the product's own language), **navigation** (can the user reach what they need without clicking item by item: grouping, filtering, jump, summary before detail, counts that make sense). Also record where it falls short of a product worth $10,000: dynamics, craft, empty/loading/failure states.
  - **Output:** a new file in `docs/review/` with findings by feature, ranked, each with its evidence (session id prefix, counts, a screenshot kept in `.local/`), then whole TASKS items (own IDs, tags, P-level, Accept lines) for every finding, those that block a UI pass first. A feature with no finding still gets its line ("checked, nothing found, on which sessions").
  - **Accept:** every inventory entry has a line; every finding is a TASKS item or an explicit "not doing, because"; the UI pass (UI-8, which absorbs UI-3, UI-4b and UI-7) and the motion passes (MOTION-1, MOTION-3) reference the findings that feed them; the ledger in the [UI pass brief](review/ui-pass-brief.md) has been read first and appended to, and every surface has its written concept answers (brief, section 3); Yash has read the ranked list. The pass finds and plans; it does not fix.
