A palette selector or token test is not the completed theme pass.
  - **26 Oct, slice 1 (measured, no CSS change needed):** on the demo session with cards expanded (code, diff, test, search, shell), no visible text falls below 4.5:1 on any of the seven palettes; the lowest is 5.3:1 (line-hit numbers on Paper), and Midnight, Mocha, Tokyo, Nord and Gruvbox stay at 7:1 or more. `tests/browser/palette-contrast.spec.ts` keeps it that way (it fails at 2.5:1 when a colour is forced down). **Slice 2 (26 Oct):** the same measurement with Settings, the Ctrl+K navigator, Search and the reading-shortcuts sheet open, on all seven palettes: no text below 4.5:1, no CSS change; a second test in the same spec guards it (it fails when dialog text is forced to a low-contrast colour). **Still open:** composer and toolbox menus, the editor, translucent compositing over the map, and the human judgement of light-theme balance on Paper, which a ratio cannot settle.

A pure black or white behind every island would give 3.8:1, but the product never paints that behind the header. Each assertion was shown to fail with the text forced to the background colour (ratios near 1.0), then restored. The colour walk now reuses one canvas, which took the existing tests from over 50 s (the overlay test timed out at 90 s on this machine) to under 3 s with identical numbers. **Still open:** the 18 px edge veil under the header is deliberately translucent, so cards passing beneath it are not held to a ratio; native `<select>` popups are drawn by the browser; the Paper line-number tone (4.71:1) is the one to watch if that palette is lightened; the human judgement of light-theme balance on Paper cannot be settled by a ratio; real recorded sessions are not covered by the synthetic fixtures.

Current Codex source succeeds; old Claude current index unavailable and explicitly qualified. Every real artifact/state is not certified.

- [x] **FILES-11 · Current files inside a nested worktree** · P2 · `files` — Yash, 3 Nov (first filed as FILES-10, an ID already used by the Markdown-size setting): a session whose root holds a git worktree under an ignored folder (here `.local/wt-v2`) shows only the lines the agent read, even in Workspace. Cause: the daemon refuses every path under `.local/` (`workspace.ts`, "not a readable workspace path"), and the worktree is not recognised as a root of its own. Proposed: recognise a linked worktree (a `.git` file pointing at this repository, as `repository-root.ts` already does for Home) under the approved root and read it with the same approval, link and protected-path rules; keep refusing `.local/` content that is not a worktree. Accept: on a real session with edits inside such a worktree, Workspace opens the whole current file at 600 and 1440; a plain `.local/` scratch file is still refused with a clear reason. **Done 4 Nov (Claude, PR #342):** a directory whose `.git` is a pointer back to this repository's `.git/worktrees` is read as a root of its own, under the same approval, link, protected-path and redaction rules, with the worktree's own index rules (its `.gitignore`, size, binary). Plain `.local/` files and another repository's worktrees stay refused. A current file the worktree no longer has (it changed branch) answers 404 "the file is no longer there" instead of a raw ENOENT 500, so the reason reads plainly. Checked on this session's own journal (Claude `rec_81c2`, 16k observations, rooted in this repository, with edits in `.local/wt-v2`): the API returns the whole current worktree file (211 lines), a `.local/audit` scratch file is refused with "not a readable workspace path", and Workspace opens the worktree file from disk with no fallback at 1440 and 600. Units: nested-worktree 2/2 plus the workspace suites (85); Workspace and Files specs 44/44. Not covered: images inside a nested worktree (the image reader keeps the `.local/` refusal).

### MOTION — interaction motion

- [x] **MOTION-1 · Every control has purposeful motion** · P2 (final pass) · `motion`
  - **4 November, complete (Claude, PR #345):** every Modern control measured in the running app (what it starts, how long, on which curve) and the gaps filled in one gated sheet (`modern/motion.css`): the Git popover and the reading windows (Git review, Git graph, file readers) open 160 ms and close 120 ms like the menus and dialogs beside them; Workspace arrives where it docks (200 ms); disclosures in menus and Settings settle in place (140 ms, no height); switches on the product curve (160 ms); press feedback eases (120 ms) on every pressable control, four of which had none; dialogs read the one `--ease-out` token. Reduced motion, low resource and keyboard stay immediate. [Inventory, rejected candidates and checks](review/motion-1-inventory.md): Claude `rec_81c2` and Codex `rec_93fd` at 600 and 1440; motion specs 280 passed. Not run: `review-animations`, which only the builder can start.
  - **19 October progress (107):** Short state changes and keyboard/reduced-motion/resource/forced-color checks added; keyboard map focus holds orbit. Modal Escape dismisses immediately with native select behavior. Main prompt/Toolbox/reader motion retained. Full per-control design pass remains open.
  - **Scope:** every clickable control and nested state on every page:
    - prompt minimize/expand, Explorer open/close, Toolbox minimize, folder expand/collapse;
    - Files ⇄ Repository ⇄ Workspace entry and return; menus, popovers, tooltips, settings sections;
    - reading windows and peek tabs; Git drawers; disclosures, toggles and segmented controls;
    - press states; copy feedback; selection.
  - **Rules:**
    - Motion is tied to the true origin and the final state: scale/opacity from the trigger for popovers, translate from the docking edge for panels, a height-free transform for disclosures.
    - Durations are about 120–240 ms with ease-out on enter and a faster exit.
    - Reversals and rapid repeated clicks are interruptible from the current state.
    - No animated virtual-row layout, no delayed or faked content.
    - Reduced-motion and low-resource variants.
  - **Method:** run `find-animation-opportunities` read-only, then `improve-animations` for a plan, implement in small commits, and finish with `review-animations`.
  - **Accept:** an inventory table (control → motion → duration → fallback) committed in a review doc; actual-session desktop checks.

- [ ] **VIZ-1 · Task-shaped visualizations and the premium bar** · P3 · `ui` `git` `content` `motion` — builder direction, 29 October. The scope grows from showing agent work as text, boxes and animation to the one place a developer uses for any task: Workspace and Files, then Git graph, Changes and branches, with the agent's work shown visually as it happens. Target: millions of people who today only read an agent's text and do not know what it is doing; the product has to feel premium end to end (worth $10,000) and be built so that nobody matches it a day later in agent visualization and developer ease at one place. Recorded now, decided after COMP-1; first the base (AUDIT-1, the UI passes).
  - **Ideas to evaluate:** (1) **compare side by side**: two branches, or a pull request's base and head, as a first-class view that works for a person working by hand and for what an agent did; (2) **a PR view beside a running agent**: while the agent works on a PR, show the PR (files, review state, what the agent has read, changed or answered) next to its work; (3) **the screen beside the work**: browser or computer use in the same window (SCREEN-1 is the foundation); (4) **task-shaped visualizations**: for different kinds of work (bug fix, refactor, review, test run, migration, dependency upgrade) a content-shaped composition that shows visually what the agent did, in the same spirit as the five signature moves; (5) one surface for a developer who works manually, with agents for some tasks, or entirely through agents.
  - **Guards:** captured evidence and the local repository first; hosted pull-request data only as an explicit opt-in, read-only call through the user's own signed-in tool, never telemetry; captured is not derived (labels stay honest); no new resident buttons (existing owners, the palette, contextual surfaces); visualization first.
  - **Output:** a ranked list with evidence source, cost and the competitor gap it closes (facts from the research ledger, none from memory), a builder decision, then whole items.

- [ ] **VIDEO-1 · Product videos: real-session demos and a comparison** · P3 · `rel` — Yash, 29 October: later, use an open-source video skill to make product videos: real-session demos and a comparison. The skill is **`/brag`** ([latent-spaces/brag](https://github.com/latent-spaces/brag), MIT, about 19,000 stars, last pushed 23 Oct 2026; Yash gave the link 29 Oct): it turns a project directory or a URL into a ~20 s launch video with music, motion and share copy, either through Hyperframes (HeyGen's HTML-to-video framework) or as `/brag-slim`, which has the model build everything with the tools on the machine. It runs inside Claude Code, Codex and others. Read on 29 Oct: its README, PRODUCT.md and `brag-slim`'s SKILL.md; nothing installed or run, and neither the full `/brag` scripts nor Hyperframes' licence checked yet.
  - **Decision pending (Yash, 29 Oct; not now):** which tool gets the best premium video of the whole product: the CLI (install and run) and the app working, in our identity. Candidates: `/brag` with Hyperframes, `/brag-slim`, a Drishya fork of either, or the other open-source options found (Remotion-based skills, claude-code-video-toolkit with Playwright demo recording). Decide by trying two or three on the same throwaway session and comparing the output against the premium bar; record the choice and the reason in the STATUS decisions log.
  - **Plan: a Drishya edition of `/brag`, forked (MIT allows it), not the stock skill.** The stock skill is the model making a generic launch video from a project's code or site; what makes ours premium is the material and the identity we feed it, so the fork changes these:
    - **Identity:** Drishya's own tokens fixed in the skill (graphite base, 1 px hairlines, one sans and one mono, GitHub-dark syntax, colour only for meaning), and only the calm tones (polished, deadpan, cinematic). The stock tones such as chaotic and yc-parody, and the brand voice of "confident, playful, ridiculous", are the wrong register for a quiet, premium product. Motion follows VISION: quiet at rest, articulate in motion, the five signature moves.
    - **Material, not mock-ups:** the video shows the real app replaying a throwaway session, recorded by a scripted Playwright run at 1440x900 (our browser-spec setup and the demo bundle already exist), and not a rebuilt or screenshot-panned UI. This is the main work and the main quality lever.
    - **Comparison template:** the same agent task seen in a terminal or the harness's own view next to Drishya's, with every statement about another product sourced from the research ledger and every number captured, never invented (the stock skill allows small illustrative UI text; ours does not allow invented claims, numbers or testimonials).
    - **Safety:** run locally only; do not use the hosted letsbrag.app (it takes a site link, and our repository is private). Public frames come only from a throwaway repository and session; the stock skill's secret and personal-data rules are kept and tightened to match AGENTS.
  - **Rules:** a public video never shows a real journal or a real repository; record a throwaway repository session made for the purpose (AGENTS: if no real session has the case, make one). Real-session screenshots stay in `.local/`. Any competitor statement in the comparison comes from the research ledger, not memory. The 30-second replay of one session is the first asset.
  - **Accept:** a replay video and a comparison video from throwaway sessions, with the sources of every claim listed; the skill's licence checked.

- [x] **TEST-2 · Rotating flaky browser specs** · P1 · `rel` — **Completed 5 November in PR #331 (awaiting other-builder review):** three consecutive complete commands on 6009c09 each pass 654 tests / 28 existing skips / zero failures/retries at DRISHYA_WORKERS=3 plus serial=1, with a normal UI build each time (810.10 / 765.74 / 745.42 s). Four-worker streak is not certified; unknown historical causes remain documented. [Completion evidence](review/test-2-live-anchor.md). **Historical investigations:** **2 November, what is left (taken over from #314 by Yash with Claude, unclaimed again):** `b2-live.spec.ts:63`, the 5 px anchor offset, which also fails on clean main, cause not verified. Everything else #314 found is fixed and merged; [report](review/test-2-live-anchor.md). **20 Oct, main cause found and fixed:** a prompt-rail chapter jump never turned follow off (keyboard navigation does), so the next projection update ran `followLatest` and pulled the view back to the bottom about 150 ms after the jump. It only showed in a warm browser, which is why `turn-fold` and `prompt-timeline` failed whenever another spec file had run first in the same worker, and passed alone. The rail now sets `follow: false`, as the "deliberate navigation stops following" rule requires (the "Back to latest" pill returns to the edge). After the fix the five-file batch (fix-chain, fresh-workspace, hover-fit, product-workspace, settings-typography) passed 117/117 twice, and the full suite on main + DATA-1/2 gave 286 passed, 1 failed. **21 Oct, fixed:** `read-before-write` failed in a full run because a pointer click on Expand moved the pointer over the card first, which can start hover reading and move the card under the click; the three specs that expand a card (`read-before-write`, `fix-chain`, `plan-checklist`) now press Expand with the keyboard and check `aria-expanded`, by the button's class since its name changes to Close (9/9 repeated). `fix-chain` expands the failed run again, as its title says. `review-questions` failed the same way (timed out on the inspector after clicking the card's ⓘ): after a pointer click expands a card, the pointer rests on its body, hover reading engages after its dwell and moves the card, so the next click misses. It now uses the keyboard too (3/3). Specs that click inside a card after expanding it should do the same. **Still open (21 Oct):** `collection-surfaces.spec.ts:48` fails on main too (focus not on the expected row); `product-workspace.spec.ts:84` (studio long changes, hover focus) and `connected-workspace.spec.ts:533` fail intermittently on main and branches. `settings-typography` failed in two full runs: modern controls transition their colour over 120 ms, and in a warm browser (any spec file run first) the spec measured the close button's ✕ mid-transition after switching palette (3.88–4.43 against 4.5). Reproduced every time with one file first; the spec now waits for running animations before measuring, and passes in that order. Also fixed: `connected-workspace.spec.ts:79` (modern) timed out on main because modern Home also lists sessions discovered on the machine and, since UI-10, pages long lists; the fixture (dated 7 Oct) fell past the first page on a machine with newer real sessions. The spec now finds it through Home's Find sessions box (18/18 pass). **Still open:** `hover-fit.spec.ts:51` (codex), **fixed 21 Oct:** the codex session mounts exactly 5 cards and the spec needs more than 4 focused, so one hover that missed its 2 s window under load failed it (line 70, `found.length > 4`). Each card now gets one retry; a card hover reading really declines declines again, so the bound is unchanged (6/6 repeated runs). Previously: it failed only in the full suite; it passes 8/8 alone and after another file, so its cause is not found yet. Original report: found 20 Oct while gating PRs #170–#173: a different handful of specs fails on each full run, and the same ones fail on plain `origin/main`, so no PR is the cause. Seen: `hover-fit.spec.ts:51` (codex), `settings-typography.spec.ts:19`, `turn-fold.spec.ts:61` (header 39442 vs 39443 px), `prompt-timeline.spec.ts:22` (833 px where ≤ 1 expected), `product-workspace.spec.ts:84` and `:777` (`toBeInViewport`, `toHaveAttribute`), and "Workspace large draft comparison" in `fresh-workspace.spec.ts`. Most pass alone and fail under the parallel full run. **Do:** reproduce each alone, with `--workers=1` and in the full run; tell test timing (waiting on a layout or paged load) from real app instability (sub-pixel layout, rail position, viewport state); fix the spec or the app; record causes here. **Accept:** three consecutive full runs with zero failures, or each remaining failure recorded with its cause. This gates every merge, so it comes before more feature work. **22 Oct (PR #196, spec-only):** four more causes fixed. (1) Pointer rests on an expanded card: `workspace-navigation:10` and `ui-recovery:17` failed when hover reading moved the card under the ⓘ click; the new `expandCard` helper in `history-gestures.ts` expands with the keyboard, and actions inside the card use `press('Enter')`. Also applied to the latent sites in `p1-pacing`, `collection-surfaces` and `typed-items`. (2) `workspace-toolbar:88` (line 115, received 0): the trace showed the wheel gestures landed before the demo's history had content, so the up-wheel did nothing and the down-wheel had nothing to scroll; the spec now waits until history can scroll. Not an app bug. (3) `captured-resources:11`: `waitForLoadState` on a `noopener` popup never got its load event under load (the routed response was served in 13 ms); it now polls the popup URL and the route hit. (4) `fresh-workspace:1766` (12 px icon/label offset): `openComposer` collected animations in the click's frame, before the opening transition started; `settled()` waits two frames first. **Overload-only, left unchanged:** under an artificial 26-thread CPU burner, `fresh-workspace:1552`, `git-review:146` and `repository-navigation:25` missed their 10 s waits (slow Home/Git/repository scans); none has failed at normal load. `collection-surfaces:48`, `prompt-timeline:22`, `fresh-workspace:1048` and `workspace-toolbar` did not reproduce alone, in their warm prefix group (3×) or under load. **Accepted 22 Oct:** after #196, three consecutive full runs on the reference machine each gave 295 passed, 26 skipped, 0 failed (15.7–16.2 min).
